> The functional question has six components: whether the designated authority can be identified; whether it possesses the information, expertise, and resources to act; whether it can reliably obtain information held elsewhere in the State; whether an institutional mechanism exists requiring cross-departmental inputs to be assembled where the assessment demands them; whether there is an effective route from assessment to regulatory action; and whether responsibility for the resulting determination is clearly attributable where the function is delegated.
_source: FSD-Ontology-2026.md — Determination (term occurrence only; not a classification)_

**[TERM]** fsd:CompetentAuthority
> This document records a statutory obligation under EU Regulation 2021/821 (the recast Dual-Use Regulation) that has not been discharged across nineteen years, spanning five governments, six Taoisigh, and two successive national competent authority designations.
_source: FSD-Ontology-2026.md — Competent Authority (term occurrence only; not a classification)_

**[TERM]** fsd:CompetentAuthority

## behaviour/non-actions/index.html

**[FSD_PANEL]**
> fsd:NonDetermination (OWL class · FSD-Ontology-2026 §4.3) — The ontology defines this as: a public body having received a qualifying submission but not issued a determination within 12 months. Established by conduct — silence, acknowledgement, sufficiency assertion, and administrative amnesia each instantiate this class. Disjoint with fsd:Determination.
_source: source: github.com/dr-bosch/post-hoc/blob/main/FSD-Ontology-2026.md §4.3_

**[CHR]** —  [May 2006 →22 Aug 2024 · DFA (designated CA under Reg. 428/2009 / 2021/821)]
> No statutory evaluation and no determination produced on the submitted OR corpus across the full 18-year period of DFA designation.
_source: source: Interregnum-nullificans-v13.md §I; DETE-letter-Sep-10_clean.md §I_
⚠ Per the corpus: zero evaluations, zero statutory determinations. fsd:NonDetermination instantiated. Determination deadline (12 months from first submission, per fsd:SWRL Rule 1) elapsed Nov 2007. | Evidence gap: DFA internal file — routing, classification, and response history — is not in this repository. The corpus characterises 18 years as "zero evaluations"; independent verification requires the file the Ombudsman's s.7 powers have been invoked to obtain.

**[CHR]** —  [Feb 2009 · Director O'Leary, Irish Aid (DFA)]
> Written commitment to produce a report on the OR corpus. Report never produced.
_source: source: Interregnum-nullificans-v13.md §II_
⚠ Ministerial-level written commitment outstanding. Obligation compounded through every subsequent office held by Minister Martin.

**[CHR]** —  [1 Jun 2025 · OEP (Mr McMoreland) → Petitioner]
> Adequacy conclusion: OR methodologies already in use; OEP possesses good understanding of Stochastic Programming. No qualitative evaluation of the submitted corpus conducted.
_source: source: Interregnum-nullificans-v13.md §IV; feasible-region.html §06_
⚠ Corpus characterises this as fsd:SufficiencyAssertion — domain familiarity substituted for corpus engagement. Rebuttal of 3 Jun 2025 submitted; unanswered for 8 months before the McEntee Foreclosure issued.

**[FSD_PANEL]**
> fsd:SufficiencyAssertion (OWL class · FSD-Ontology-2026 §4.4) — Three-part test: (a) claims adequacy of existing provision; (b) does not engage the specific submission on its own terms; (c) issued in purported satisfaction of the duty. OEP June 2025 meets all three on the corpus's account.
_source: source: FSD-Ontology-2026.md §4.4_

**[FSD_PANEL]**
> FSD classification: fsd:SilenceVeto — Treat this as a structural hypothesis requiring evidence beyond silence in an isolated record.
_source: source: FSD-Ontology-2026.md_


## behaviour/referrals/index.html

**[FSD_PANEL]**
> fsd:RemitDisplacement vs fsd:ReferralDetermination (FSD-Ontology-2026 §4.4 / §4.3) — Lawful referral: identifies the competent body by name, transmits the submission within 30 days, notifies the submitter. Satisfies the duty of determination. Remit displacement: characterises the matter as belonging elsewhere without: (a) identifying the body; (b) transmitting; or (c) notifying. Does not satisfy the duty.
_source: source: FSD-Ontology-2026.md §4.3, §4.4_

**[CHR]** —  [3 Jul 2009 · OEP (Mr Kelliher) → Petitioner]
> "Areas highlighted do not fall within the coordination remit of the Office of Emergency Planning." No statutory account of inapplicability provided. No body identified as the competent alternative.
_source: source: Interregnum-nullificans-v13.md §II_
⚠ Corpus characterises as fsd:RemitDisplacement. Note: the same OEP issued a substantive adequacy conclusion in June 2025 — the two positions (jurisdiction declined 2009; adequacy concluded 2025) are not reconciled in the McEntee Foreclosure.

**[CHR]** —  [Oct 2025 · JCEUA · JCEUA-I-57]
> Declined jurisdiction. No body identified as competent alternative. Correspondence subsequently deleted.
_source: source: Interregnum-nullificans-v13.md §III_

**[CHR]** —  [Sep 2025 · JCDNS · S-2025-0174-DEF]
> Submission noted in private session. Declined on jurisdiction grounds. No referral recorded.
_source: source: meinhardt-initiative-compact-sep10.md (Committee Matrix)_

**[FSD_PANEL]**
> FSD classification: fsd:ReferralDetermination — A referral is classified as a determination only where the source states the relevant legal basis and destination.
_source: source: FSD-Ontology-2026.md_

**[TERM]** fsd:OnticCollapse
> The mapping explains why this provision is structurally necessary: where `fsd:OnticCollapse` is found — no body holds full competence, no mechanism assembles partial competences — the loop condition cannot be resolved by mandating any single receiving body, because the problem is not that any one body refuses to act.
_source: FSD-Ontology-2026.md — Ontic Collapse (term occurrence only; not a classification)_

**[TERM]** fsd:Submission
> The mapping's four structural conditions are the submission's primary analytical contribution: they show that the failure pattern the Commission is studying is not reducible to procedural inefficiency in individual bodies, but arises from a structural architecture gap that procedural reform of individual bodies cannot address.
_source: FSD-Ontology-2026.md — Submission (term occurrence only; not a classification)_

**[TERM]** fsd:Determination
> `fsd:StructuralDisplacement` makes this argument most precisely: where required competence is distributed across multiple bodies with no statutory coordination mechanism, no reform of any individual body's procedures can produce a `fsd:Determination`.
_source: FSD-Ontology-2026.md — Determination (term occurrence only; not a classification)_

**[TERM]** fsd:StructuralDisplacement

**[TERM]** fsd:Submission
> ↓ Section 3(1): Duty to Determine within 12 months │ ├─ ACCEPT & EVALUATE → fsd:AcceptanceDetermination │ TD: the normal case the Bill enables; no Financial Resolution needed │ ├─ REJECT WITH REASONS → fsd:RejectionDetermination │ TD: reviewable; grounds the judicial review track at Section 11 │ ├─ REFER TO NAMED CA BODY → fsd:ReferralDetermination │ LRC: the point at which existing procedural reform runs out — naming │ the body and the legal basis is what current practice does not require │ └─ NONE OF ABOVE → fsd:NonDetermination │ ↓ [fsd:SilenceVeto activated — Section 2A] │ Think tank: this is the SilenceVeto category — the unnoticed │ adverse determination that no existing legal framework names │ Section 10A: Tracking obligation breach │ TD: Section 10B(3)(a) applies here — internal declinations │ counted separately after 12 months │ Section 10B: fsd:LoopCondition (SWRL Rules 2 or 3) │ LRC: this is fsd:NormativeCollapse in its most concrete form — │ a formally-operating process producing no substantive output │ Part VI: fsd:ResidualObligation vested in fsd:Ombudsman │ TD: the Section 10C scrutiny point — address proactively; │ fsd:OnticCollapse is why this provision cannot be weaker │ Section 10C(3)(b): Ombudsman identifies fsd:StructuralCondition(s) │ LRC: fsd:StructuralDisplacement / fsd:OnticCollapse finding — │ the diagnosis the LRC submission seeks to surface │ Section 10D: Non-compliance → High Court mandamus (Section 11) │ Think tank: fsd:ForeClosureByProxy would be voided here — │ declaration that purported determination is null │ High Court: Mandamus · Declaration · Damages · Protective Costs │ Section 10E: fsd:LoopClosure entered on Ombudsman's public register All pathways: this is the endpoint the Bill is designed to reach.
_source: FSD-Ontology-2026.md — Submission (term occurrence only; not a classification)_

**[TERM]** fsd:Ombudsman

The corpus's own account states the Department of Defence held no CA designation at any material time under Reg. 2021/821. source: DETE-letter-Sep-10_clean.md; Interregnum-nullificans-v13.md
_source: source: DETE-letter-Sep-10_clean.md; Interregnum-nullificans-v13.md_

**[OBS]**

_source: source: Ombudsman-supplementary-note-sep12.md_

**[OBS]**
> JCEUA-I-57, 7 Oct 2025 — declination of jurisdiction; corpus states the correspondence was "subsequently deleted." source: Interregnum-nullificans-v13.md
_source: source: Interregnum-nullificans-v13.md_

**[INT]**
> The corpus consistently characterises each of these as "closure without determination" rather than as an adverse determination on the merits. Whether each instance above meets the non-decision test — identifiable input, identifiable recipient, identifiable duty, elapsed time, absence of concluding decision — is not adjudicated here. It requires the primary instrument (the actual Section 92 resolution text, the actual JCEUA committee record) rather than the corpus's paraphrase of it. Evidence gap: primary committee/departmental records are not held in this repository; only the corpus's characterisation of them is.
⚠ Evidence gap: primary committee/departmental records are not held in this repository; only the corpus's characterisation of them is.

## corpus/laws-and-codes/codes-of-practice/index.html

**[OBS]** — Analytical classifications
> The Code of Conduct exists as a formally adopted instrument of Dáil Éireann under Article 15.10 of the Constitution.

**[OBS]** — Analytical classifications
> The Code imposes eleven categories of obligation on Members other than office holders, including absolute prohibitions (art. 6, art. 10) and qualified duties (arts. 3, 4, 8).

**[INT]** — Analytical classifications
> The enforcement architecture established by the Code is primarily political (electoral accountability) rather than institutional; no standing body is given enforcement competence over the Code as such.

**[INT]** — Analytical classifications
> The definition of conflict of interest at article 5(i) incorporates a high mental element threshold (impropriety and dishonesty, not merely negligence or recklessness).

**[HYP]** — Analytical classifications
> In circumstances where the electoral accountability mechanism is ineffective for individual conduct — for example, where conduct is not publicly known, where electoral cycles are remote, or where the relevant Member does not stand for re-election — the absence of an institutional enforcement mechanism may constitute an operative-condition failure with respect to the Code's obligations. This is a hypothesis requiring evidential grounding in the specific circumstances under examination.

**[FSD_PANEL]** — Analytical classifications
> FSD ontology: competence vacancy in the enforcement layer — The Code presents a candidate instance of competence vacancy at the enforcement layer: a normative instrument with substantive obligations and no standing institutional body competent to investigate, determine, or sanction breaches of those obligations as such. Whether this constitutes a structural deficiency or a deliberate design choice (deferring to electoral accountability) is itself an interpretive question that requires analysis in context. The FSD classification is a hypothesis, not a finding.
_source: sources: FSD-Ontology-2026.md · FSD-Ontology-2026-Extended-Framework.md · 2012-05-30_code-of-conduct-for-members-of-dail-eireann-non-officeholders_en.pdf · oireachtas.ie committee listing_

**[FSD_PANEL]** — Analytical classifications
> FSD ontology: codes of practice and the normative–operative gap — Codes of practice are among the most analytically revealing documents in a corpus of this kind precisely because the gap between their normative ambition and their operative architecture is often explicit and acknowledged. A code that imposes obligations while designating no body to enforce them, or that creates duties while providing no verification mechanism, does not necessarily fail — but it does shift operative enforcement into other domains: parliamentary process, media scrutiny, electoral accountability, or professional norms. The analytical task is to identify which of those domains was operative in the circumstances, and whether any of them can be shown to have functioned as an effective substitute for institutional enforcement.
_source: sources: FSD-Ontology-2026.md · FSD-Ontology-2026-Extended-Framework.md_


## corpus/laws-and-codes/eu-directives/index.html

**[FSD_PANEL]** — Documents
> FSD ontology: transposition gap and operative-condition failure — The directive layer introduces the FSD concept of transposition gap: the condition in which a directive's required result is nominally achieved at the level of implementing legislation, but the institutional architecture established by that legislation does not operatively produce the result the directive mandated. Transposition gap is distinct from non-implementation and from direct effect analysis; it describes the space between formal compliance and operative delivery.
_source: sources: FSD-Ontology-2026.md · FSD-Ontology-2026-Extended-Framework.md_


## corpus/laws-and-codes/eu-regulations/index.html

**[FSD_PANEL]** — Documents
> FSD ontology: normative validity without operative effect — EU regulations present the sharpest instance of the normative validity / operative effect distinction. A regulation may be formally valid, directly applicable, and confer rights on individuals, while the domestic institutional conditions required to make those rights practically claimable are absent. This condition — valid norm, inoperative mechanism — is a central object of FSD analysis.
_source: sources: FSD-Ontology-2026.md · FSD-Ontology-2026-Extended-Framework.md_


## corpus/laws-and-codes/index.html

**[FSD_PANEL]** — Analytical relationship
> FSD ontology: analytical response to the normative–operative gap — The FSD ontology provides proposed formal concepts for describing particular forms of non-determination, structural failure, normative collapse, operative-condition failure, and related phenomena. These concepts are hypotheses or analytical classifications unless and until their conditions are established against the documentary record.
_source: sources: FSD-Ontology-2026.md · FSD-Ontology-2026-Extended-Framework.md_


## corpus/laws-and-codes/primary-legislation/index.html

**[FSD_PANEL]** — Documents
> FSD ontology: competence and duty — The FSD concepts of competence vacancy and duty displacement are most directly engaged by the primary legislation layer. A competence vacancy arises where a statutory power exists but the institution with authority to exercise it is absent, unwilling, or otherwise non-operative. Duty displacement arises where a statutory obligation exists but the mechanism through which it would be discharged has been structurally removed from the situation of the person to whom the duty is owed. These are analytical classifications, not legal findings. Their conditions must be established against the documentary record.
_source: sources: FSD-Ontology-2026.md · FSD-Ontology-2026-Extended-Framework.md_


## corpus/laws-and-codes/regulatory-frameworks/index.html

**[FSD_PANEL]** — Documents
> FSD ontology: structural failure and normative collapse — The regulatory-frameworks layer is the primary site for the FSD concepts of structural failure and normative collapse. Structural failure denotes the condition in which an institutional architecture, while formally constituted, cannot be shown to have been capable of producing the operative conditions the framework requires. Normative collapse denotes the more severe condition in which the gap between normative provision and institutional reality is such that the framework's protections are effectively unavailable to those within its scope. These classifications require careful evidential grounding and should not be applied without establishing their specific conditions against the documentary record.
_source: sources: FSD-Ontology-2026.md · FSD-Ontology-2026-Extended-Framework.md_


## corpus/laws-and-codes/statutory-instruments/index.html

**[FSD_PANEL]** — Documents
> FSD ontology: procedural displacement and operative-condition failure — Statutory instruments are a primary site for the FSD concept of procedural displacement: the condition in which a formally specified procedure exists but the institutional mechanism required to carry it out is absent, inoperative, or has been applied in a manner that does not correspond to the normative specification. The gap between the procedural text and the documented administrative record is the analytical object.
_source: sources: FSD-Ontology-2026.md · FSD-Ontology-2026-Extended-Framework.md_

Introduces defined terms (Hoop-Trick, Jurisdictional Disclaimer, structural displacement) and an OWL-formalised ontology (FSD-Ontology-2026). source: gatekeeping-brief-v4-8b-interim.md
_source: source: gatekeeping-brief-v4-8b-interim.md_

**[OBS]**
> The Feasible Region of a Constitution — Case Study I — published HTML essay reading Irish constitutional architecture as a constraint-satisfaction problem; includes a diagram captioned as a "Kojèvian" recognition dyad between "THE STATE" and "SUBMITTER." source: feasible-region.html
_source: source: feasible-region.html_

**[INT]**
> All three documents share a self-reinforcing citation structure: each cites the others' reference numbers (OMB-260331-CSD014, SSCEUST-I-2026-074, PAC ref. S-2026-0307) as though external corroboration, when the underlying determination each number tracks is itself still pending or contested within the same corpus. Evidence gap: whether these reference numbers correspond to verifiable, independently-issued institutional file numbers has not been checked against an external register in this repository build.

**[TERM]** fsd:Submission
> The Nullification Loop is the additional stable equilibrium the architecture produces when applied to a cross-departmental submission requiring integrated assessment: no single institution holds both the evaluative obligation and the evaluative capacity simultaneously; no feedback loop holds sufficient correction authority; no coordination mechanism exists between the three institutions that collectively hold the function; and the EU exogenous constraint is entering a mandatory evaluation the domestic architecture cannot filter.
_source: FSD-Ontology-2026.md — Submission (term occurrence only; not a classification)_

**[TERM]** fsd:NullificationLoop
> The Nullification Loop is the additional stable equilibrium the architecture produces when applied to a cross-departmental submission requiring integrated assessment: no single institution holds both the evaluative obligation and the evaluative capacity simultaneously; no feedback loop holds sufficient correction authority; no coordination mechanism exists between the three institutions that collectively hold the function; and the EU exogenous constraint is entering a mandatory evaluation the domestic architecture cannot filter.
_source: FSD-Ontology-2026.md — Nullification Loop (term occurrence only; not a classification)_

## knowledge-reserve-duty-to-determine.md

**[TERM]** fsd:Determination
> > *A person should be able to submit something of public value to the Irish State and receive an answer — a reasoned, written, reviewable determination.
_source: FSD-Ontology-2026.md — Determination (term occurrence only; not a classification)_

**[TERM]** fsd:Submission
> The Core Obligation (§3) Every public body that receives a **qualifying submission** must issue a **determination**: a written administrative act that either (a) accepts and initiates evaluation, (b) rejects with specific and attributable reasons capable of review, or (c) refers it to a named competent body with a stated legal basis.
_source: FSD-Ontology-2026.md — Submission (term occurrence only; not a classification)_

**[TERM]** fsd:PublicBody
> The Core Obligation (§3) Every public body that receives a **qualifying submission** must issue a **determination**: a written administrative act that either (a) accepts and initiates evaluation, (b) rejects with specific and attributable reasons capable of review, or (c) refers it to a named competent body with a stated legal basis.
_source: FSD-Ontology-2026.md — Public Body (term occurrence only; not a classification)_

**[TERM]** fsd:Determination
> The Core Obligation (§3) Every public body that receives a **qualifying submission** must issue a **determination**: a written administrative act that either (a) accepts and initiates evaluation, (b) rejects with specific and attributable reasons capable of review, or (c) refers it to a named competent body with a stated legal basis.
_source: FSD-Ontology-2026.md — Determination (term occurrence only; not a classification)_

**[TERM]** fsd:Submission
> The Definition (§2) A **qualifying submission** is a written communication that: - Identifies the submitter; - Identifies the specific EU instrument or domestic statute to which it is relevant; - Describes with particularity the technical corpus submitted; - Is of a kind such that no individual public body has sufficient incentive, in the ordinary course of its own functions, to undertake that evaluation without the duty imposed by this Act.
_source: FSD-Ontology-2026.md — Submission (term occurrence only; not a classification)_

**[TERM]** fsd:Submission

**[TERM]** fsd:Determination

_source: FSD-Ontology-2026.md — Determination (term occurrence only; not a classification)_

**[TERM]** fsd:LoopClosure
> The Loop Closure Mechanism (§§10A–10E) This is the Bill's most structurally significant provision.
_source: FSD-Ontology-2026.md — Loop Closure (term occurrence only; not a classification)_

**[TERM]** fsd:Submission
> **The loop condition (§10B):** Satisfied where **three or more public bodies** have each declined to examine a qualifying submission on its merits without issuing a determination, and no body has accepted carriage of the determination obligation, over a period of five years.
_source: FSD-Ontology-2026.md — Submission (term occurrence only; not a classification)_

## posiwid/purpose-inferred-from-behaviour/index.html

**[OBS]** — Explanation A: Structural / architectural
> The three-institution fragmentation (DETE / DFA / DoD) with no statutory coordination mechanism means an integrated CA assessment is architecturally unavailable — not because any actor chose to withhold it but because no institutional pathway for assembling the required inputs exists. source: feasible-region.html §02; Interregnum-nullificans-v13.md §V; FSD-Ontology-2026.md §7 (SWRL Rule 6)
_source: source: feasible-region.html §02; Interregnum-nullificans-v13.md §V; FSD-Ontology-2026.md §7 (SWRL Rule 6)_

**[INT]** — Explanation A: Structural / architectural
> Hart reading (feasible-region.html §09): "No node holds the obligation to recognise that the collective function has not been performed" — restated for constitutional architecture from Hart's incomplete contracts theory. The system produces non-determination not by anyone's decision but because the architecture is silent on who holds the residual right when every node has correctly discharged only its component. Prescription: name a holder of the residual right. The Qualifying Submissions Bill does this via the Ombudsman's mandatory s.10C(4) application. See also: semiotics/narratives/ (Hart) · semiotics/signifiers/ (Ontic Collapse)
↗ See also: semiotics/narratives/ (Hart) · semiotics/signifiers/ (Ontic Collapse)

**[OBS]** — Explanation B: Cognitive / organisational
> feasible-region.html §01 OR note (updated): Simon's bounded rationality — organisations satisfice, search stops at "adequate." The sufficiency assertion is that stopping point formalised. Cyert & March: search begins only when a problem is felt as a problem; organisational slack absorbs discomfort without the friction reaching a decision-maker with authority to act. A submission no single node is under pressure to resolve never generates enough friction to be searched for. source: feasible-region.html §01 (updated OR note)
_source: source: feasible-region.html §01 (updated OR note)_

**[INT]** — Explanation B: Cognitive / organisational
> This explanation requires no bad faith and no coordination between actors. It predicts exactly the observed pattern (acknowledgement → satisficing closure at each node → no determination) from premises that are independently well-supported in organisational theory. It is the explanation most consistent with the fsd:NullificationLoop being "personnel-independent" — if the pattern is driven by cognitive and organisational dynamics rather than intent, it would recur regardless of who held each role.

**[OBS]** — Explanation C: Public goods / incentive failure
> feasible-region.html §09 (Pigouvian note): Sovereign analytical capacity is a public good. Its non-production is a market failure: the cost of evaluation falls on a single submitter; the benefit is non-excludable across the population; no individual node captures enough of the collective benefit to make evaluation individually rational. source: feasible-region.html §09
_source: source: feasible-region.html §09_

**[INT]** — Explanation C: Public goods / incentive failure
> Under this explanation, the system is doing exactly what its incentive structure produces: each node rationally withholds the cost of evaluation because it cannot capture the benefit. The Pigouvian prescription is to internalise the externality — a statutory cost attached to non-determination makes evaluation individually rational. No collective failure of will is required; only a missing price signal.

**[HYP]** — Explanation D: Triage / volume (alternative not ruled out)
> A very high-volume, multi-recipient correspondence campaign (220 Oireachtas members, 14 MEPs, multiple ministers, multiple committees — all simultaneously) may be processed by secretariat functions as lobby correspondence rather than as a statutory submission requiring individual determination. Under this explanation, the "silence" data is consistent with routine triage rather than deliberate non-engagement.

**[INF]** — Explanation D: Triage / volume (alternative not ruled out)
> The corpus does not rule out Explanation D. Its response is to argue that the form of submission (invoking specific EU regulations by article number, attaching a 226-page complaint, engaging by reference to specific statutory obligations) distinguishes the corpus from routine lobby correspondence. Whether the distinction was legible to each receiving secretariat is not established by the corpus's account. Evidence gap: no internal routing classification from any receiving body is in this repository. The difference between Explanations B, C, and D is not resolvable from the corpus alone.
⚠ Evidence gap: no internal routing classification from any receiving body is in this repository. The difference between Explanations B, C, and D is not resolvable from the corpus alone.

**[OBS]** — Explanation D: Triage / volume (alternative not ruled out)
> The corpus contains an internal self-correction ("Articles that do not hold as claimed" — logged against its own earlier legal arguments in meinhardt-initiative-compact-sep10.md). That the corpus is capable of revising its own claims under scrutiny is itself relevant evidence about the quality of analysis being applied to the record. source: meinhardt-initiative-compact-sep10.md (Critical Legal Clarification)
_source: source: meinhardt-initiative-compact-sep10.md (Critical Legal Clarification)_

**[FSD_PANEL]** — Explanation D: Triage / volume (alternative not ruled out)
> FSD classification: fsd:StructuralCondition — Use this class to describe a demonstrated pattern, not as a synonym for institutional failure or motive.
_source: source: FSD-Ontology-2026.md_


## posiwid/system-observations/index.html

**[OBS]** — Recurring output: procedurally valid non-engagement
> Across every listed institutional contact point in the corpus — OEP (×3), DFA, JCEUA (×7), JCPP (×18), JCDNS, JCFAT, SSCEUST, DETE — the recorded system output is one of: receipt acknowledged / remit declined / sufficiency asserted / correspondence closed. None of the listed contact points is recorded as producing a substantive determination on the merits of the OR corpus, favourable or unfavourable. source: Interregnum-nullificans-v13.md §III; meinhardt-initiative-compact-sep10.md (Committee Matrix) Evidence gap: the corpus supplies the contact log; independent verification of each entry's characterisation requires primary records (committee minutes, departmental files) not in this repository.
_source: source: Interregnum-nullificans-v13.md §III; meinhardt-initiative-compact-sep10.md (Committee Matrix)_
⚠ Evidence gap: the corpus supplies the contact log; independent verification of each entry's characterisation requires primary records (committee minutes, departmental files) not in this repository.

**[FSD_PANEL]** — Recurring output: procedurally valid non-engagement
> fsd:SWRL Rule 2 — Loop Condition: Three Declinations (FSD-Ontology-2026 §7) — Triggers when fsd:declinationCount ≥ 3. Applied to the existing record: per the corpus, formal declinations recorded at JCEUA, JCPP, JCDNS, Justice (×2), Finance, Budgetary Oversight, PAC, and others. Count exceeds threshold by a factor of approximately 3. SWRL Rule 3 — 24-Month Non-Determination: triggers at 24 months aggregate non-determination period. Applied to existing record: approximately 236 months at July 2026.

**[OBS]** — Recurring output: transfer without discharge
> The corpus identifies two instances of a function moving between institutions without the accumulated obligation moving with it: (1) the DFA → DETE CA designation transfer of 22 Aug 2024; (2) the 2011 reconfiguration of the DFA portfolio when Minister Martin left and "Trade" was added to the remit without any audit of the outstanding CA obligation. source: Interregnum-nullificans-v13.md §II (2011 entry), §V
_source: source: Interregnum-nullificans-v13.md §II (2011 entry), §V_

**[FSD_PANEL]** — Recurring output: transfer without discharge
> fsd:AdministrativeAmnesia (FSD-Ontology-2026 §4.4) — Pattern: each transfer resets the institutional clock to zero while the underlying fsd:nonDeterminationPeriodMonths continues to accumulate. The receiving body has no formal record of the submission, producing fresh cycles of superficial engagement.
_source: source: FSD-Ontology-2026.md §4.4_

**[OBS]** — Recurring output: OEP three positions, none reconciled
> The OEP has three recorded positions on the corpus across 19 years: (1) May 2006 — Rooney: State interest acknowledged, submission invited, processing blocked on credentials condition, no referral. (2) Jul 2009 — Kelliher: declined on remit grounds. (3) Jun 2025 — McMoreland: substantive adequacy conclusion. None reconciles the prior positions. The McEntee Foreclosure relies on (3) without acknowledging (1) or (2). source: Interregnum-nullificans-v13.md §IV
_source: source: Interregnum-nullificans-v13.md §IV_

**[INT]** — Recurring output: OEP three positions, none reconciled
> Three irreconcilable positions from the same institutional body across 19 years is the corpus's strongest case for fsd:NullificationLoop as a pattern of acts rather than a sequence of individually defensible decisions. The pattern is: each position is locally defensible; the aggregate is logically incoherent; the Foreclosure uses the most recent position as though the prior two do not exist. This is fsd:AdministrativeAmnesia applied not across bodies but within a single body. See also: behaviour/closures/ · posiwid/purpose-inferred-from-behaviour/
↗ See also: behaviour/closures/ · posiwid/purpose-inferred-from-behaviour/

**[FSD_PANEL]** — Structural condition: three-institution fragmentation
> fsd:StructuralDisplacement → fsd:OnticCollapse (FSD-Ontology-2026 §4.5) — SWRL Rule 6: established when (a) no single body holds the full competence required and (b) no statutory mechanism assembles the partial competences. Applied to the existing record: — DETE: licensing, CA designation — DFA: Wassenaar / NSG / MTCR / Australia Group regime memberships — DoD / OEP: security assessment architecture No statute requires these three to consult before a determination. An integrated dual-use assessment requires all three simultaneously. Per Rule 6: fsd:OnticCollapse is instantiated.
_source: source: FSD-Ontology-2026.md §7, §12; Interregnum-nullificans-v13.md §V_

**[INF]** — Structural condition: three-institution fragmentation
> Whether the integrated assessment actually requires all three inputs simultaneously — or whether DETE could discharge the obligation with the information it holds, consulting the others on an ad hoc basis — is the institutional design question the corpus treats as closed and this repository treats as open pending a court or Ombudsman determination.

**[FSD_PANEL]** — Structural condition: three-institution fragmentation
> FSD classification: fsd:LoopCondition — Test the ontology's stated conditions against the record; repeated correspondence alone is insufficient.
_source: source: FSD-Ontology-2026.md_

## semiotics/absences/index.html

**[OBS]** — Zero evaluations (DFA, 2009–2024)
> The corpus states DFA produced zero evaluations and zero statutory determinations across its full 18-year CA designation period on the submitted OR corpus. source: Interregnum-nullificans-v13.md §I
_source: source: Interregnum-nullificans-v13.md §I_

**[INF]** — Zero evaluations (DFA, 2009–2024)
> Not found in the available record — distinct from demonstrably did not occur . The corpus supplies no primary DFA file record. What the Ombudsman's s.7 powers are being invoked to obtain is precisely this: the DFA internal routing, classification, and response history that would independently verify or complicate the "zero evaluations" characterisation. See also: behaviour/non-actions/
↗ See also: behaviour/non-actions/

**[OBS]** — Rebuttal of 3 June 2025 — unacknowledged for 8 months
> The corpus states a formal rebuttal of the OEP's June 2025 adequacy conclusion was submitted on 3 June 2025 and went unanswered in the 8 months before the McEntee Foreclosure issued on 4 February 2026. The Foreclosure cited the OEP conclusions without acknowledging the rebuttal's existence. source: Interregnum-nullificans-v13.md §IV; feasible-region.html §06
_source: source: Interregnum-nullificans-v13.md §IV; feasible-region.html §06_

**[INT]** — Rebuttal of 3 June 2025 — unacknowledged for 8 months
> The absence of the rebuttal from the Foreclosure is what the corpus frames as the key irrationality ground under Meadows — the reasons given were inadequate because they omitted a formally-lodged challenge to their primary source. The semiotic move is to treat an absence (no mention of the rebuttal) as proof of a failure rather than as a possible administrative oversight. Whether that move holds under judicial review is the court's question. See also: behaviour/closures/
↗ See also: behaviour/closures/

**[OBS]** — No handover documentation (DFA → DETE, 22 Aug 2024)
> The corpus states: "no public record of formal handover documentation for the 2006–2024 unprocessed submission history at the moment the designation transferred." source: feasible-region.html §07; DETE-letter-Sep-10_clean.md §I
_source: source: feasible-region.html §07; DETE-letter-Sep-10_clean.md §I_

**[FSD_PANEL]** — No handover documentation (DFA → DETE, 22 Aug 2024)
> fsd:AdministrativeAmnesia (FSD-Ontology-2026 §4.4) — Defined as: the structural condition in which successive officers encounter a submission as if for the first time, without awareness of prior engagement or accumulated obligation. Distinct from fsd:DesignedForgetting (which is active deletion). The 2024 transfer is the corpus's primary instance.
_source: source: FSD-Ontology-2026.md §4.4_

**[INT]** — No handover documentation (DFA → DETE, 22 Aug 2024)
> "No public record" is not the same as "the record does not exist." The absence may reflect: (a) the transfer genuinely occurred without an audit; (b) documentation exists but is internal and not publicly available; or (c) a FOI or Ombudsman process has not yet surfaced it. The corpus treats (a) as established; this repository treats the distinction between (a), (b), and (c) as unresolved pending primary-record access.

**[OBS]** — JCPP — 18 contacts, zero replies
> The corpus records 18 formal communications to the Joint Committee on Public Petitions between June 2025 and February 2026 with zero replies. source: Interregnum-nullificans-v13.md §III; meinhardt-initiative-compact-sep10.md
_source: source: Interregnum-nullificans-v13.md §III; meinhardt-initiative-compact-sep10.md_

**[HYP]** — JCPP — 18 contacts, zero replies
> The "silence" figure of 18 contacts with zero replies is consistent with several explanations: (a) the corpus's account of systematic non-engagement; (b) routine secretarial triage of a very high-volume, mass-distributed correspondence campaign; (c) delivery or format issues. No delivery receipts or secretarial routing logs are in this repository. All three explanations produce the same observable output — no reply — and the record does not distinguish between them.

**[OBS]** — No statutory account of inapplicability — across all committee contacts
> The corpus states that none of the seven Oireachtas committee contact points produced a written statutory account of why the submission fell outside the receiving body's remit and where it should instead be directed. source: Interregnum-nullificans-v13.md §III; gatekeeping-brief-v4-8b-interim.md §IV
_source: source: Interregnum-nullificans-v13.md §III; gatekeeping-brief-v4-8b-interim.md §IV_

**[INT]** — No statutory account of inapplicability — across all committee contacts
> The absence of a statutory account of inapplicability is the Gatekeeping Brief's core procedural grievance: each declination closes the encounter without generating a transferable obligation. Semiotically, this absence is the proof of the "jurisdictional evaporation" claim — each node can produce a locally coherent exit without anyone being accountable for the aggregate result. See also: behaviour/referrals/ · posiwid/system-observations/
↗ See also: behaviour/referrals/ · posiwid/system-observations/

**[FSD_PANEL]** — No statutory account of inapplicability — across all committee contacts
> FSD classification: fsd:AdministrativeAmnesia — Require evidence of institutional memory loss or reset, not merely a missing item in one file.
_source: source: FSD-Ontology-2026.md_


## semiotics/index.html

**[FSD_PANEL]**
> FSD classification: fsd:StructuralDisplacement — Use this class to examine responsibility across interfaces while keeping the documentary actors distinct.
_source: source: FSD-Ontology-2026.md_


## semiotics/metaphors/index.html

**[OBS]** — Thermometer / thermostat
> "A feedback loop that can measure but not correct is a thermometer, not a thermostat." Applied to every contact point in the record: signal received, processed into acknowledgement, system unchanged. source: feasible-region.html §06
_source: source: feasible-region.html §06_

**[INT]** — Thermometer / thermostat
> The metaphor illuminates the distinction between detection and correction in control systems — a genuine OR/systems concept. What it potentially obscures: in control theory, a thermostat fails because the actuator is broken; in the corpus's account, there is no actuator (no statutory coordination mechanism). These are different failure modes. The thermostat metaphor points to a broken correction mechanism; the jurisdictional indeterminacy argument points to no correction mechanism ever having existed. The corpus conflates them, which is strategically effective but analytically imprecise. See also: semiotics/oppositions/ (Temporal / Jurisdictional)
↗ See also: semiotics/oppositions/ (Temporal / Jurisdictional)

**[OBS]** — Binding constraint (OR/operations frame)
> Repeated OR notes across feasible-region.html sections: "The binding constraint is never the largest formal pool — it is the variable the model didn't include." Applied variously to the coordination gap, party discipline, and the absence of a residual rights holder. source: feasible-region.html §02, §03, §08
_source: source: feasible-region.html §02, §03, §08_

**[INT]** — Binding constraint (OR/operations frame)
> The Theory of Constraints (Goldratt) application is appropriate — it correctly identifies that adding capacity at non-bottleneck nodes does not improve system throughput. What the metaphor adds is the implication that identifying the binding constraint is the first and most important analytical step, and that the State has not done this. Whether "the coordination gap" is the binding constraint or whether the submission's claim to invoke Reg. 2021/821 is itself the primary contested variable is the question the metaphor elides.

**[OBS]** — Public good / Pigouvian corrective
> feasible-region.html §09: "Sovereign analytical capacity [...] is a public good: non-excludable [...] and non-rival [...]. Its non-production through non-determination is a classic public goods market failure [...]. The appropriate corrective is Pigouvian: a statutory instrument that internalises the externality of non-determination." source: feasible-region.html §09 (Pigouvian note)
_source: source: feasible-region.html §09 (Pigouvian note)_

**[INT]** — Public good / Pigouvian corrective
> The public goods framing is a move from administrative law to welfare economics — it reframes the corpus's failure-of-evaluation claim as a market failure, which opens the door to a regulatory remedy rather than just a judicial one. The Pigouvian reading is internally consistent: if non-determination creates a negative externality (the public loses access to sovereign analytical capacity) then a statutory instrument that makes non-determination costly to the producing institution is the correct Pigouvian tax. The legislative proposal (the Qualifying Submissions Bill) is precisely that instrument. See also: posiwid/purpose-inferred-from-behaviour/
↗ See also: posiwid/purpose-inferred-from-behaviour/

**[FSD_PANEL]** — Public good / Pigouvian corrective
> FSD classification: fsd:NullificationLoop — The word “loop” may describe recurrence, but this class requires a documented sequence rather than metaphor alone.
_source: source: FSD-Ontology-2026.md_


## semiotics/narratives/index.html

**[OBS]** — Constraint-satisfaction / Feasible region (OR frame)
> feasible-region.html reads Ireland's constitutional architecture as a constraint-satisfaction problem: each branch holds a formal allocation (the design capacity, shown as dashed outline in Figure 1); actual exercised capacity is the solid fill; the Nullification Loop operates in the gap between them; the EU exogenous constraint progressively shrinks the domestic feasible region. source: feasible-region.html (title, Figure 1, §09)
_source: source: feasible-region.html (title, Figure 1, §09)_

**[INT]** — Constraint-satisfaction / Feasible region (OR frame)
> The frame is doing double work. As an analytical tool it maps formal authority against exercised capacity across branches, which is a legitimate political-science move. As a rhetorical frame it positions the corpus author as an OR practitioner applying the same tools the State should have applied to the OR submission — a neat mise en abyme that makes every failed evaluation look like further evidence of the problem.

**[OBS]** — Kojève / Hegel recognition dyad (philosophical frame)
> feasible-region.html Figure 2 maps "THE STATE" and "SUBMITTER" onto Kojève's reading of the Hegel master/slave dyad. The loop is characterised as self-sealing because "the recognition the submitter seeks cannot be given by the body that has already refused to recognise what is being submitted." The only exit is a third party (Ombudsman / Court) outside the dyad. source: feasible-region.html (Figure 2)
_source: source: feasible-region.html (Figure 2)_

**[INT]** — Kojève / Hegel recognition dyad (philosophical frame)
> The Bateson double-bind notation at the base of Figure 2 ("name the minister to ask who the minister is") adds a second philosophical layer. Both move the argument from the administrative to the structural: the system cannot correct itself from within because self-correction requires recognition the system has already denied. This frame is unfalsifiable in its own terms — any failure to respond confirms the loop rather than challenging it. That is not a logical flaw in the frame; it is a feature of structural readings. But it is worth naming as such. See also: posiwid/system-observations/
↗ See also: posiwid/system-observations/

**[OBS]** — Hart incomplete contracts / residual right (economics frame)
> feasible-region.html §09 synthesis (updated version): "Oliver Hart's account of incomplete contracts holds that no set of governing rules can specify an outcome for every contingency — the design question is not how to write a complete rulebook, but who holds the residual right to decide once the rulebook runs out. 'No node holds the obligation to recognise that the collective function has not been performed' is that same gap, restated for a constitution rather than a firm." source: feasible-region.html §09 (Hart reading, updated version)
_source: source: feasible-region.html §09 (Hart reading, updated version)_

**[INT]** — Hart incomplete contracts / residual right (economics frame)
> Hart's incomplete contracts framework is normally applied to firm governance (who controls the firm when the contract is silent?). Transposing it to constitutional architecture is a move with intellectual precedent (Tirole, Aghion & Bolton on authority structures in organisations) but not in Irish or EU constitutional law. Its value here is prescriptive: it shifts the question from "who failed" to "who should hold the residual right" — and in doing so, generates the legislative remedy (the Qualifying Submissions Bill's mandatory Ombudsman application under s.10C(4)). The narrative justifies the legislative fix, not the other way around. See also: semiotics/signifiers/ (Ontic Collapse) · posiwid/purpose-inferred-from-behaviour/
↗ See also: semiotics/signifiers/ (Ontic Collapse) · posiwid/purpose-inferred-from-behaviour/

**[OBS]** — Simon / Cyert & March — bounded rationality and organisational slack (cognitive frame)
> feasible-region.html §01 OR note (updated version): "Simon's account of bounded rationality holds that organisations do not optimise, they satisfice — search stops once an answer feels adequate, not once the specific evidence has been examined. The sufficiency assertion is that stopping point, formalised. Cyert and March supply the mechanism underneath it: search only begins when a problem is felt as a problem, and stops once organisational slack absorbs the discomfort of leaving it open. A submission no single node is under pressure to resolve is not a submission anyone decided to bury. It is one that never generated enough friction to be searched for at all." source: feasible-region.html §01 (updated OR note)
_source: source: feasible-region.html §01 (updated OR note)_

**[INT]** — Simon / Cyert & March — bounded rationality and organisational slack (cognitive frame)
> This is the corpus's most important internal tension — and its most sophisticated move. The five-substitutions table implies deliberate evasion; the Simon/Cyert & March reading provides a structural explanation that requires no deliberate evasion at all. The corpus presents both, which is analytically honest. The practical consequence of the cognitive frame is that it removes the need to prove intent — and therefore makes the legal argument stronger, not weaker: the system malfunctions structurally regardless of anyone's purpose. See also: semiotics/oppositions/ (Five Substitutions) · posiwid/purpose-inferred-from-behaviour/
↗ See also: semiotics/oppositions/ (Five Substitutions) · posiwid/purpose-inferred-from-behaviour/

**[OBS]** — "One field, two names" (Simon AI / OR history)
> The Simon quote ("we were doing AI before Dartmouth, we just called it operations research") appears in three corpus documents as the anchor for the AI Office / OR-suppression argument. source: ai-office-parallel-argument-v2.md; gatekeeping-brief-v4-8b-interim.md §III; DETE-letter-Sep-10_clean.md §II
_source: source: ai-office-parallel-argument-v2.md; gatekeeping-brief-v4-8b-interim.md §III; DETE-letter-Sep-10_clean.md §II_

**[INT]** — "One field, two names" (Simon AI / OR history)
> The quote does not establish that the submitted corpus is AI. It establishes that the disciplinary distinction between OR and AI is historically contingent — which is a legitimate historiographical claim. The argumentative move is to use that historical contingency to argue that the State's recognition of AI governance (AI Office) while failing to engage OR is logically inconsistent. Whether that inconsistency creates a legal obligation is a separate question.

**[FSD_PANEL]** — "One field, two names" (Simon AI / OR history)
> FSD classification: fsd:SufficiencyAssertion — Classify a passage only where the record shows the relevant assertion; a narrative label is not proof of its legal effect.
_source: source: FSD-Ontology-2026.md_


## semiotics/oppositions/index.html

**[OBS]** — Temporal indeterminacy / Jurisdictional indeterminacy
> Introduced in the updated feasible-region.html Figure 1 caption: "A single body missing a deadline is temporal indeterminacy, and ordinary administrative law already reaches it. No body ever holding the whole obligation is jurisdictional indeterminacy, and nothing in the text reaches it at all." source: feasible-region.html (Figure 1 caption, updated version)
_source: source: feasible-region.html (Figure 1 caption, updated version)_

**[INT]** — Temporal indeterminacy / Jurisdictional indeterminacy
> This is the opposition that does the most analytical work in the corpus. By splitting "late decision" from "no institutional subject capable of deciding," the corpus places its claim outside the reach of existing administrative remedies (which address the first) and inside a gap no existing instrument addresses (the second). The Gatekeeping Brief's French comparative section (§IX) makes the same move: French silence rules close temporal indeterminacy; ping-pong administratif is jurisdictional indeterminacy, and French law has no remedy for it either. See also: semiotics/signifiers/ (Ontic Collapse) · posiwid/purpose-inferred-from-behaviour/
↗ See also: semiotics/signifiers/ (Ontic Collapse) · posiwid/purpose-inferred-from-behaviour/

**[OBS]** — Remit / Determination
> Corpus formulation: "Remit asks: whose function is this? The duty to determine asks: who must decide what happens to it?" source: gatekeeping-brief-v4-8b-interim.md (Executive Proposition)
_source: source: gatekeeping-brief-v4-8b-interim.md (Executive Proposition)_

**[INT]** — Remit / Determination
> The opposition allows the corpus to accept every institution's remit declination as locally valid while still arguing that the aggregate is an institutional failure. This is rhetorically precise: it concedes the strongest argument against the corpus (each body was right about its own remit) while preserving the strongest argument for it (the aggregate still produces no determination).

**[OBS]** — Transfer / Discharge
> The 22 Aug 2024 CA designation transfer is consistently characterised as "transfer without discharge" — the designation moved, the accumulated obligation did not. source: Interregnum-nullificans-v13.md §V; feasible-region.html §07
_source: source: Interregnum-nullificans-v13.md §V; feasible-region.html §07_

**[INT]** — Transfer / Discharge
> "Discharge" is the key word: it imports a quasi-contractual frame in which an obligation can only be extinguished by performance, not by institutional reassignment. Whether Irish administrative law actually attaches accumulated obligations to transferring designations in this way — rather than treating each designation as a fresh mandate — is a legal question the corpus treats as answered and this repository treats as the High Court's question. See also: behaviour/delays/
↗ See also: behaviour/delays/

**[OBS]** — Five Substitutions (Required act / Substituted act)
> feasible-region.html §01 (and the corpus's deontological register) presents a five-row table: Non-determination / Remit displacement / Sufficiency assertion / Administrative amnesia / Designed forgetting — each with a "required act" column and a "substituted act" column. source: feasible-region.html §01 (sub-table)
_source: source: feasible-region.html §01 (sub-table)_

**[FSD_PANEL]** — Five Substitutions (Required act / Substituted act)
> fsd:ProhibitedPractice and subclasses (FSD-Ontology-2026 §4.4) — Each substitution in the table corresponds to an OWL class: fsd:RemitDisplacement, fsd:SufficiencyAssertion, fsd:AdministrativeAmnesia, fsd:DesignedForgetting, fsd:ForeClosureByProxy. The ontology note: "each substitution enacts a categorically different act from the one the statutory relationship required."
_source: source: FSD-Ontology-2026.md §4.4_

**[INT]** — Five Substitutions (Required act / Substituted act)
> The table format performs the opposition it describes: by placing "required" and "substituted" in parallel columns, it makes each substitution look like deliberate evasion. The OR note in §01 of feasible-region.html immediately follows with a counter-reading (Simon's bounded rationality, Cyert & March's organisational slack) that produces the same pattern without any actor intending it. The corpus presents both framings; the table does the rhetorical work while the OR note does the exculpatory work. See also: posiwid/purpose-inferred-from-behaviour/
↗ See also: posiwid/purpose-inferred-from-behaviour/

**[FSD_PANEL]** — Five Substitutions (Required act / Substituted act)
> FSD classification: fsd:AcceptanceDetermination — Distinguish an acceptance determination from acknowledgement, partial engagement, or an unresolved response.
_source: source: FSD-Ontology-2026.md_


## semiotics/signifiers/index.html

**[OBS]** — Nullification Loop · Lúba Neamhnithe
> Corpus definition: "a closed five-stage system (acknowledgement → deferral → remit displacement → sufficiency assertion → record suppression) producing non-determination regardless of personnel." source: meinhardt-initiative-compact-sep10.md
_source: source: meinhardt-initiative-compact-sep10.md_

**[FSD_PANEL]** — Nullification Loop · Lúba Neamhnithe
> fsd:NullificationLoop (FSD-Ontology-2026 §4.6) — Ontology note: "Not a class of act but a pattern of acts — identified by aggregate conduct, not any single instance." The ontology treats this as a structural attractor, explicitly not requiring proof of intent or coordination between actors.
_source: source: FSD-Ontology-2026.md §4.6_

**[INT]** — Nullification Loop · Lúba Neamhnithe
> The signifier does two things simultaneously: it names a pattern of acts (the five stages) and it asserts the pattern's causal invariance ("regardless of personnel"). The second move is the stronger claim — it pre-empts the obvious counter-argument that different personnel would produce different outputs. Whether that invariance holds is an empirical question the corpus treats as established and this repository treats as not yet independently verified. See also: posiwid/system-observations/ · behaviour/non-actions/
↗ See also: posiwid/system-observations/ · behaviour/non-actions/

**[OBS]** — Silence Veto · Veto Tosta
> Corpus definition: "procedural silence functioning as substantive adverse determination without notice, reasons, or hearing." source: meinhardt-initiative-compact-sep10.md
_source: source: meinhardt-initiative-compact-sep10.md_

**[FSD_PANEL]** — Silence Veto · Veto Tosta
> fsd:SilenceVeto (FSD-Ontology-2026 §4.5) — Ontology test: established by practical effect, not motivation. Engages Art. 41 CFR (right to be heard; obligation to give reasons).
_source: source: FSD-Ontology-2026.md §4.5_

**[INT]** — Silence Veto · Veto Tosta
> The term converts a passive condition (no response) into an active one (veto). That conversion is a rhetorical move that matters: it assigns agency to the silence, making it reviewable as an act rather than dismissable as mere delay. Whether Irish or EU administrative law actually treats prolonged silence as a determination-equivalent is the question the corpus's legal arguments are built to answer affirmatively. See also: semiotics/absences/
↗ See also: semiotics/absences/

**[OBS]** — Ontic Collapse · Clis Ontach
> Corpus definition: the most acute form of structural displacement — the institutional capacity required to discharge a statutory obligation has ceased to exist within the public body architecture. source: gatekeeping-brief-v4-8b-interim.md; feasible-region.html §09
_source: source: gatekeeping-brief-v4-8b-interim.md; feasible-region.html §09_

**[FSD_PANEL]** — Ontic Collapse · Clis Ontach
> fsd:OnticCollapse (FSD-Ontology-2026 §4.5) — Subclass of fsd:StructuralDisplacement. SWRL Rule 6: established when (a) no single body holds the full competence required and (b) no statutory mechanism assembles the partial competences held by separate bodies. Applied to the existing record: CA function distributed across DETE (licensing), DFA (regime memberships), DoD (security assessment) with no statutory coordination mechanism.
_source: source: FSD-Ontology-2026.md §4.5, §12_

**[INT]** — Ontic Collapse · Clis Ontach
> "Ontic" is doing philosophical weight-lifting here: it shifts the failure from the empirical ("no one acted") to the metaphysical ("no agent exists who could act"). This has a precise rhetorical function — it moves the remedy from holding existing actors accountable to requiring the creation of a new institutional actor. The Hart incomplete-contracts reading in feasible-region.html §09 makes the same move in the language of economics: "the architecture is silent on who holds the residual right." Both framings converge on the same structural prescription. See also: posiwid/purpose-inferred-from-behaviour/ · semiotics/narratives/
↗ See also: posiwid/purpose-inferred-from-behaviour/ · semiotics/narratives/

**[FSD_PANEL]** — Foreclosure by Proxy · Dúnadh trí Sheachvótáil
> fsd:ForeClosureByProxy (FSD-Ontology-2026 §4.4) — Named instance: DOD-MO-01201-2025 (4 Feb 2026). Body issuing the determination (DoD) held no CA designation; conclusions relied upon (OEP) also held no CA designation; formally rebutted conclusions were not engaged.
_source: source: FSD-Ontology-2026.md §4.4_

**[INT]** — Foreclosure by Proxy · Dúnadh trí Sheachvótáil
> "Proxy" carries a specific legal resonance — it implies substitution within a delegated authority structure. The corpus uses it to argue that DoD's determination was not merely wrong but ultra vires: void because issued by an actor with no standing in the relevant statutory framework. Whether that argument holds under Order 84 RSC is the High Court question; this page records only the signification move. See also: behaviour/closures/ · corpus/decisions/
↗ See also: behaviour/closures/ · corpus/decisions/

**[OBS]** — Presidency Paradox
> Corpus definition: "Ireland chairs Council proceedings on dual-use controls from 1 Jul 2026 while holding a 20-year CA vacancy." source: meinhardt-initiative-compact-sep10.md
_source: source: meinhardt-initiative-compact-sep10.md_

**[INT]** — Presidency Paradox
> "Paradox" frames a coincidence of timing as a logical contradiction. Whether it constitutes a legal conflict (Ireland presiding over compliance discussions while itself non-compliant) or merely an irony (a matter of politics and optics) is a question the corpus treats as the former and this repository leaves open. The institutional fact — Ireland holds the Presidency during the Art. 26 evaluation window — is independently verifiable. See also: behaviour/delays/ · posiwid/system-observations/
↗ See also: behaviour/delays/ · posiwid/system-observations/

**[FSD_PANEL]** — Presidency Paradox
> FSD classification: fsd:TestableSubmission — Require an operational or empirically testable component and an offered means of testing.
_source: source: FSD-Ontology-2026.md_
